44. **The split row's remove link in the Next version pen (Sid, 2026-10-04; Mark, on the iPad and iPhone after quick 261004-eoi: "the remove link is visible on the first shared ingredient, but not on the others. Whole milk in Step 2 has remove link. Whole milk in Step 3 does not have remove link. Seems like something Sid needs to review and suggest options."; drawn, awaiting Mark's look; nothing here is approved).** Board `split-remove-link.html`; generators `.planning/canvas-generators/splitlink.py`, `splitlink-capture.mjs`, `splitlink-measure.mjs`.
**Finding: design, not a defect, and not what quick 261004-eoi changed.** Measured in the built app (WebKit, the dist of 2026-10-04; 1600 fine pointer, 1366 and 393 coarse; Olive Oil v1 with Next version open): the pen's table has 14 lines with a grams field; 12 carry a remove link and 2 do not, and the two that do not are exactly the second lines of the two split ingredients, **Whole milk in Step 3 (250.4 g) and Sucrose in Step 3 (64 g)**. The result is identical at all three widths and both pointers. `IngredientTable.jsx` (line 638) renders the control only for `portionIndex === 0`, with the comment "exactly one control per row"; the orphan flag has the same rule ("only on the row's first portion line, never once per portion", line 264). Quick 261004-eoi (commits d9d1a72, c49eec6) only moved the link before the portion line on that first line. So the link on Whole milk in Step 2 and none in Step 3 is the build doing what its code says; what the sketch never drew is a split ingredient's second line. Decision 26 and `1600-remove-link.html` drew one split row with one link.
**What the link does on a split ingredient.** Remove is a toggle on the ingredient (`onTogglePenRowRemoved(row.id)`), not on a line: pressing it on the Step 2 line strikes **both** lines (the name, the grams and the percentage on each) and the link there turns to "restore"; the Step 3 line has no control and nothing on it says that the Step 2 link reaches it. The two lines are in different step groups, about 350px apart at 1366 (Whole milk's rows at 1059 and 1408 on the page) and further at the phone, so a maker looking at Step 3 sees no way to take the milk out and no sign of where it is.
**What is drawn** (the built app's own markup, WebKit; rows per width; one pair of panels per option, resting and after the link is pressed; the crop is the Step 2 head to the last Sucrose line, 611px at 1600 and 1366, 677 at 393 as built). *A, as built:* 12 links; pressing Whole milk's strikes both lines. *B (recommended):* the link on every line of a split ingredient, 14 links; every one removes the ingredient, so after it both lines strike and both read "restore". *C:* the link on every line, each removes only its own line: resting identical to B; after the link on the Step 3 line only that line strikes (drawn on that line; its percentages are not recomputed, the build has no such state). The links keep the same position: after the name and the estimated tag, before the portion line, the 14px gap; each added link adds 14.4px of height to the line (the split line grows from 57 to 71, the height Step 2's line already has), 28.8px for the two in Olive Oil v1.
**Recommendation (Sid; Mark decides): B.** Every line a maker might want to remove has the control; the word, position and effect are the one the build already has, so nothing is new in the data model and the pen's test for the first line still holds; and the restore is as findable as the remove. Its cost is that pressing "remove" on a Step 3 line strikes a line in Step 2, off screen at the phone: the strike on the line pressed and the percentages moving are the visible sign, and "restore" sits on the same line. A leaves the asymmetry Mark found. C reads most literally ("remove this line") but it is a new behaviour: the data model removes an ingredient, not a portion; a portion removal changes the split and the ingredient's total, and a maker who wants less milk in Step 3 can already set its grams to 0. Not drawn: a plain-words cue on the later line instead of a link (for example "removed with Step 2's"): it adds a sentence to every split line and still leaves no control where the eye is.
**What it touches:** no approved decision is reversed (26's link position is unchanged). **App change brief (for `/gsd-quick` after Mark approves B; nothing here edits `app/`).** `IngredientTable.jsx` line 638: render `RemoveRowControl` on every portion line of a split row, not only `portionIndex === 0` (the orphan flag stays on the first line); the tests that pin one control per row, and the cases quick 261004-eoi added (Tests A to D, c49eec6: the order on the first line, the non-split row), change with it; the control's accessible name should read the ingredient and the line ("remove Whole milk, Step 3") so two links with one name are not two buttons a screen reader cannot tell apart. Verification: the board against the build, the pen at 393 on the iPhone.
**Found while drawing, said plainly.** (1) **A removed split row's portion lines read a share that is not real:** after "remove" on Whole milk, its two portion lines read "120 g of 370.4 g · 86.3% in all" and "250.4 g of 370.4 g · 86.3% in all" (the same figure; 46.3% before), i.e. the ingredient's grams against the batch without it. The struck percentage in the right column is right; the portion line is not. A defect in the build, separate from this decision; it shows in the A and B "after remove" panels as built. (2) The grams field on the Step 3 line clips at the 353 and 528 widths ("250.4" shows as "250.": an existing narrow-field clip in the pen). (3) Not verified on the iPad or iPhone: Mark's report is what the device shows and the build agrees with it in WebKit on a Mac. (4) C's after-remove panel is constructed from the app's own removed-row markup.
**Questions for Mark:** (1) A, B or C? (2) Is the portion line's 86.3% on a removed split row (finding 1) a fix for the same quick, or its own? **Mark's answers (2026-10-04):** (1) B, a link on every line, each removes the ingredient. (2) The 86.3% fix goes in the same quick as the link.

45. **The print page break for the ingredients table, by steps (Sid, 2026-10-04; Mark's answer to decision 36's print question: "Always PC; if the ingredients table doesn't fit on one page, then break the table by Steps, so that when the ingredients are grouped into steps, all of the ingredients print on the same page. Also, the Ingredients table header should repeat on the next page."; drawn, awaiting Mark's look; nothing here is approved).** Board `before-you-start-print-break.html`; generators `.planning/canvas-generators/breaks.py`, `breaks-capture.mjs`, `breaks-measure.mjs`; the Chrome check `breaks-pdf-check.py` with `pdf-pages.swift`.
**The rule, as drawn.** PC always: Before you start opens the formula page, above the table. If the table does not fit what is left of the page, it breaks **between step groups only**: a step head and its rows are one unit and never split; the table's header row (Ingredient, % of batch) repeats at the top of the next page, in the same columns; the Total row is the table's last row and goes with the last group, never alone under a repeated header and never printed above the break. Rows with no step head (a flat table, Standard Base v2) are units of one row. The Ingredients heading goes with the table when the whole table moves. A group taller than a page (none is seeded: the longest, Mexican Chocolate v3's Step 1, is 12 rows and about 423px of 968) would split between rows.
**What is drawn** (the built app's own markup at letter width, as decision 36's print board drew it: 816 x 1056, margins 48 / 56 / 40, 968px usable, the batch layer removed, no Phase 4 geometry (no footer, tick boxes or ruled As made column), so these are best cases for fitting; WebKit for the layout, Chrome for the print check; the blue bars in the left margin and the blue lines in the bottom margin are the drawing's, not the page's).
- *Row 1, Olive Oil v1, where the break falls.* PC as it stands: content 1,050px against 968 usable, 82px over (Graza Drizzle crosses the margin line, Soy lecithin and the Total fall past it). **Break by steps:** page 3 holds the title, Before you start and Steps 2, 3 and 6, and ends 46px above the foot (Allulose's row); Step 8 (its head and two rows, 97px) is 51px too tall for that, so it starts the next page whole. Page 3, continued: the repeated header, Step 8, the Total (168px in all), and then, in the drawing, the Instructions (K1 below).
- *Row 2, after the table's tail (question 1).* **K1 (recommended):** the Instructions follow the tail on the same page, whole steps only: steps 1 to 7 of 10 fit, the other three go on the next page, so Olive Oil v1 takes 2 pages after the formula page. **K2:** the tail has a page of its own (about 17% full) and the Instructions open the next one, as they do when the table fits: 3 pages after the formula page.
- *Row 3, the repeated header (question 2).* Header only, as Mark asked (recommended), or "Ingredients, continued" over it.
- *Row 4, a recipe that fits.* Mexican Chocolate v3 with the two seeded notes added (constructed: only Olive Oil v1 has notes): content 777px against 968, 191px to spare. No break, no repeated header, the Total closes the table; the Instructions open page 4, as decision 36's PC drew it.
- *Row 5, the edge (question 3).* Many versions put every ingredient under one step. Mexican Chocolate v3 with the seeded notes three times over (constructed, to fill the page): Step 1 does not fit under Before you start (1,034 against the 1,016 margin line), so the rule moves the whole table, with its heading, to the next page and page 3 ends after Before you start with 503px empty. The alternative is what a browser does with no rule: it splits the group where the foot falls, here after the tenth ingredient, and the last two rows and the Total go to the next page under no step head.
**Verified in a real print (Chrome headless, `--print-to-pdf`, the same markup with the rules below, letter, the same margins; text read per page with PDFKit).** Olive Oil v1: page 1 ends after Step 6, page 2 begins "INGREDIENT % OF BATCH, STEP 8 ..." (header repeated, Step 8 whole); with the Instructions after the table, page 2 ends after step 7 and page 3 holds steps 8 to 10: the K1 drawing exactly. Mexican Chocolate v3 with the notes three times: the group moves whole with the rule and splits (Cinnamon and Allulose on the next page) without it. Chrome's default already moves Step 8 for Olive Oil v1, only because it will not leave one row alone at a foot; it splits a group whenever the foot falls inside one with enough rows after it. **Not verified: iPad and iPhone Safari's print** (Playwright WebKit cannot print to a PDF here); `tbody { break-inside: avoid }` and a repeating `thead` are the two things to confirm on the device.
**Recommendation (Sid; Mark decides): K1, header only, the whole group moves.** K1 saves a page (2 against 3 after the formula page for Olive Oil v1) and a page that is 17% full reads like a printing fault; its cost is one more rule (the Instructions open a page unless the table's tail is on the page above). Header only is Mark's own words and the foot Phase 4 draws (recipe, version line, code, page n of m) already names the page. The whole group moves because it is the rule as Mark stated it ("all of the ingredients grouped into a step print on the same page"); the cost is a half-empty page 3 for a one-step recipe with long notes.
**What it touches:** no approved decision is reversed; it answers decision 36's "PC does not fit" consequence and settles that PC is not conditional (PA and PB are dropped). The earlier print board `before-you-start-print.html` stays as the record of PA, PB and PC. **Documents to update once approved (not edited):** `.impeccable/surfaces/route-print-recipe-sheet.md` § 3 (the formula page: PC, and the break by steps with the repeated header), Phase 4's `04-CONTEXT.md` (D-06's "head of the method" becomes "head of the formula page"; a new decision for the break), `DESIGN.md`'s print entries, and the two todos on the list. **App change brief (Phase 4's print route; nothing here edits `app/`):** (1) one `tbody` per step group in the print table (the screen's table has one `tbody` with the step heads as rows; the print route has its own tree), `tbody { break-inside: avoid }`, `thead { display: table-header-group }`, **and `tfoot { display: table-row-group }`: Chrome repeats a `tfoot` on every page by default (measured: the Total printed at the foot of page 1, above the break), which is wrong for a total**; (2) the region headings keep with what follows, `.region-name { break-after: avoid }` (measured in Chrome: without it the Ingredients heading stays alone at the foot of page 1 and the table starts page 2); (3) the Total row stays with the last group (it is in its own `tfoot`: give the last `tbody` and the `tfoot` one wrapper, or lay the pages by measurement); (4) if Mark answers K1, no forced break before the Instructions when the table breaks, and the steps break between steps; if K2, the forced break stays; (5) if Safari does not honour `break-inside` on a `tbody`, the print route lays the pages by measurement, as this board does: the brief already has the route render the pages as they print; (6) Phase 4's tick boxes, ruled As made column and footer add height, so more versions will break: the rule does not depend on the geometry; (7) verify by printing Olive Oil v1 and Mexican Chocolate v3 to PDF and reading the pages, as above.
**Found while drawing, said plainly.** (1) Decision 36's board said the table's last row crosses the foot; to the pixel, Graza Drizzle crosses it and Soy lecithin and the Total are past it. (2) Two traps for the build, both measured in Chrome: the Total repeats at every page foot unless the `tfoot` is a plain row group, and a heading is left alone at the foot unless it keeps with what follows. (3) K1 makes the Instructions' page break depend on where the table ended; Mark has not seen that rule. (4) The edge case and the fits case are constructed from the seeded notes; no seeded one-step version has notes. (5) The numbers are WebKit's layout at 816 wide; Chrome agrees on the breaks, not measured to the pixel. (6) Not verified on Mark's iPad or iPhone.
**Questions for Mark:** (1) After the table's tail, do the Instructions follow on the same page (K1, recommended) or open the next page (K2)? (2) The repeated header alone (recommended) or with "Ingredients, continued" over it? (3) When one step holds the whole table and it does not fit, move the whole table to the next page (recommended) or split it between rows? **Mark's answers (2026-10-05):** (1) K1: the Instructions follow the table's tail on the same page. (2) The repeated header alone, no "Ingredients, continued". (3) When one step holds the whole table and it does not fit, split it between rows (not move the whole table). Together with decision 36's PC and the break by steps, that is the print rule: Before you start opens the formula page; if the table does not fit it breaks between step groups, the header repeats, the Total goes with the last group, and a one-step table that does not fit splits between rows. Build: Phase 04 / the Before you start phase (the `tfoot { display: table-row-group }` and `.region-name { break-after: avoid }` findings in decision 45 apply).

46. **The other wide and phone boards, redrawn from the build with the dots and the one 10px radius (Sid, 2026-10-04; Mark's answer to decision 41, question 2: "Redraw the other boards with the dots now", and decision 38's one radius; drawn, awaiting Mark's look; nothing here is approved).** Generators `.planning/canvas-generators/final.py` (the wide boards), `phone.py` (the phone boards), `pen.py` (the record pen's boards), with `final-capture.mjs`, `phone-capture.mjs`, `final-calibrate.mjs`, `phone-measure.mjs`; the check is `redraw-conform.mjs` and `redraw-conform-open.mjs`; one pass of everything is `redraw-all.sh`.
**What the build was when it was read** (WebKit, coarse pointer, the preview server on :4173, nothing started or built; the dist moved under the work while quick batch 261004-ox1 landed, and the captures were taken again after the last landing, the dist of 19:16): the dots from 724 (261004-igr), the sticky header, the fly-out and the rail (ly8), the one 10px radius and the rail's weight 600 (ly7), the Why flush and in the hand (ly6), and from ox1 the phone tab's weight 600 (ox2), the details' one 8px row gap (ox3), the record pen's skin and cues (ox4), the dots at every width and the batch head's 16px row gap (ox5), a remove link on every line of a split ingredient (ox6), the phone table's plan / As made / struck (ox7, P3), the table's grid from 724 (ox8, D3), the Go to batch row from 724 to 1365 (ox9) and Balance and Watch for open from 984 (oxa). **So the final design of decision 33 and the dots of decision 41 are now all built, and nothing is drawn on top of the build any more:** `final.py` and `phone.py` detect each part in the app's own CSS and add the drawing's rule only where the build lacks it; at this reading none is added.
**What was redrawn.** *Wide, 17 boards (final.py):* `744-batch`, `834-batch`, `983-batch`, `984-batch`, `1024-batch`, `1366-batch`, `1600-batch`, `1600-no-batch`, `1600-pen`, `1600-long-history`, `1920-batch`, `724-1365-go-to-batch`, `1600-remove-link`, the three `-sticky-nav` boards and `1600-sticky-rail`. *Phone, 7 boards (phone.py):* `393-batch`, `723-batch`, `393-phone-log`, `723-phone-log`, `393-show-changes-head`, `723-show-changes-head` and `393-all-folded`. *The record pen, 5 boards (pen.py):* `350-pen-app`, `393-pen-app`, `724-pen-range`, `740-pen-range`, `759-pen-range`: their skin read two tokens that decision 38 retired (`--app-notebook-field-radius`, `--app-radius-action`), so a regeneration would have drawn square corners; it reads `--app-radius-control` and the fields, joined stops and segments and actions are 10px (they were 8 and 10). These five keep their 2026-10-02 markup: the build's own pen (ox4) now also carries decision 40's cues and the Clear fix, which `record-pen-app` and `record-pen-cues` draw; `350-pen-as-built` is the reference and is untouched.
**How, and what was retired.** The boards' markup is the build's own (`final-capture.json` and the new `phone-capture.json`, taken from the build). Quick 261004-ly8 built the header, the menu button and the fly-out, so the header markup injected on 2026-10-03 (`with_menu`) and the candidate rules HDR, G and HDR724 of `nav-candidates.css` are retired (the boards read `shell.css` as the build does); `D3`, `P3`, `JUMP` and the dots' block stay in the generators for the day a part is missing. The sticky-nav boards keep their one drawing aid (a drawing has no window, so the panel, the scrim and the bar are placed by hand) on the build's own classes (`shell__rail--open`, `shell__scrim`). The phone boards were hand-drawn pages (`gen.py`, `phonelog.py`, `allfolded.py`, `showchangeshead.py`: History as a `history-disclosure` text control, the Go to batch row and the batch head as hand markup, square tab places, the counts at the far end, Olive Oil drawn with a second version the app does not have); they are replaced by captures of the build at 393 and 723. The print boards of decision 45 resolve the app's CSS as a print page (`resolve_media(..., screen=False)`), so the screen-only D3 grid does not reach them.
**Conformance, board against build, measured** (`redraw-conform.mjs`, WebKit, coarse pointer: the same page opened in the build and the board's panel with the same setup; 41 selectors (up to 14 elements each) and 26 computed values, from the shell's top left; 0.6px on a box; strict, with no allowance for an unbuilt part): **33 panels on 15 boards (`744`, `834`, `983`, `984`, `1024`, `1366`, `1600-batch`, `1920`, `1600-no-batch`, `1600-pen`, `724-1365-go-to-batch`, `393-batch`, `723-batch`, `393-phone-log`, `723-phone-log`), 46,336 checks, 0 off.** The one thing allowed to differ is the phone's tab row `y` (fixed to the window's foot in the app, to the panel's foot in a drawing). The fly-out drawn open against the build's own open fly-out (`redraw-conform-open.mjs`, 744 x 1133, 984 x 768 and 1366 x 954, scrolled as the boards are): 609 checks, 0 off (the panel's and the scrim's `position` and the bar's `sticky` are placed by hand in a drawing; the menu button's padding is its `:hover` after the tap). Widths, the table and name columns, Balance and the log beside or below, and wraps are the same as before to 0.1px in all 21 panels of `final-board-measure.json`; only page heights moved (Mexican Chocolate v3 at 1366: 2,310 to 2,316 with the hand Why; Olive Oil v1 at 1600 and 1366: 2,822 to 2,814; Olive Oil v1's pen at 1600: 4,309 to 4,238 with the one title field; Mexican Chocolate v3's pen: 2,534 to 2,473). The same check on the ten wide page boards as they stood before this redraw (the previous snapshots, `git show HEAD:`) finds **748 differences in 28,231 checks** (the rail's and the tab row's places square, the Why indented 40px in the text face, the counts at the far end of every fold row, the batch head's 32px both ways, the pen's fields at 8px and a single title as two). **Not individually checked:** `1600-long-history` (eight versions are constructed), the sticky closed and rail boards (the same capture and CSS as the checked ones), `1600-remove-link`, the two show-changes-head boards, `393-all-folded` (Ingredients, Instructions and Batch are closed with the fold row's own markup, because they do not fold in the build) and the five pen boards.
**Not redrawn, and why.** (1) The five count boards (`versions-1-vs-many`, `batches-0-and-1`, `batches-many`, `batches-many-393`, `upright-393`): decided option boards (decision 22) drawn from hand markup and constructed states (one version, two or more batches: none is seeded); they still show the counts at the far end. (2) `393-show-changes`, `723-show-changes`, `393-show-changes-cases`, `393-pen-changes`, `723-pen-changes` and the option boards of decisions 24, 25, 30 and 31 (`list-form-options`, the two ingredient-option boards, the ladder and nav boards): tables and pens in the Sheet's square grammar or old option sheets, no label row and no App radius (the phone table's P3 is built now and those boards draw the same figures). (3) The boards already drawn on the build of 2026-10-04 by their own decisions: `record-pen-app`, `record-pen-cues`, `info-labels-*`, `why-row`, `app-radius`, `version-details-rhythm`, `split-remove-link`, `sheet-title-pen`, `before-you-start-*`. Sid recommends leaving the count boards as the record of the options; redrawing them needs constructed states, a drawing and not a capture.
**Found while redrawing, said plainly.** (1) The old 1920 board's Why wrapped to three lines in a board and two in the build, because the boards take Caveat from the Google link (offline here); the check now supplies the app's own font file and the two agree. (2) At 393 and 723 the build has no History row for Olive Oil v1 (one version: "Only this version so far"); the old hand-drawn boards showed one, with a draft version 2 the seed does not have, so the phone boards show Mexican Chocolate v3 for History and Olive Oil v1 for the tasted batch. (3) Decision 30's fourth panel (the band alone with Show changes beside Next version) is gone: option 3 put Show changes at the Ingredients head, as built. (4) `1600-remove-link` was two panels (as built against proposed); the build now is the proposal, so it draws the build's Step 2 stretch and its Step 3 stretch, where decision 44's link on every line now stands. (5) `cssscope.py` now takes a `screen` flag: the app's D3 block is `@media screen and (min-width: 724px)`, and a print board must not read it (the print boards would otherwise have drawn the grid). (6) The redrawn boards carry what the build does, and three of those are already rows on Mark's List from the quick batch: the table head reads on two lines from 724 (INGREDIENT over AS MADE and % OF BATCH, because the As made head's box is 0.55px wide), the phone pen's share-only rows drop their share 8px when edited, and Mexican Chocolate v3 shows 325px of empty space under its table at 984 to 1365 with Balance open (the side column is 1,433px tall and the grid gives the surplus to the ingredients row). Board and build agree on each, so none is a conformance gap; each is a design question for its own row. (7) Device-unverified: everything here is Playwright WebKit and system Chrome on a Mac; nothing was tried on the iPad or iPhone.
**Questions for Mark:** (1) Leave the five count boards as the record of the options (recommended), or redraw them with constructed states? **Mark's answers (2026-10-05):** (1) Leave them as the record of the options; they are not redrawn.

48. **The ingredients table's head on one line from 724 (Sid, 2026-10-05; Mark's answer to the ox8 decide row, decision 47 (2): "one head line"; and his check on the iPad and the Mac: "table header (Ingredient) is indented (I starts at about N in ingredients); As Made is wrapped and starts at left edge of the table"; the one-line head is decided, the redrawn boards are drawn and awaiting Mark's look; Ingredient's start (A or B below) is not decided; nothing else here is approved).** Generators `.planning/canvas-generators/final.py` (the wide boards; the rule is the section `ONEHEAD` of `ingredient-options.css`, added only where the build lacks it, by `built_onehead`) and `onehead.py` (the option board `table-head-one-line.html`), with `onehead-measure.mjs`, `onehead-probe.mjs` and `onehead-opts.mjs` (WebKit, coarse pointer, and Chrome for the probe, from the preview server on :4173, the dist of 21:00:30, nothing started or built); the check is `redraw-conform.mjs` with `INJECT=onehead`.
**What Mark saw, measured in the build** (Olive Oil v1 and Mexican Chocolate v3 with a batch, 724 to 1920; WebKit and Chrome agree to 1px). (1) *"Ingredient" starts under the N of "Ingredients".* It starts 72px after the heading's first letter (152 against the heading's 80 at 1366; the heading's last "n" is at 149), because the head is one cell over the amount and the names (decision 15) and, with a batch, the As made column (56px and a 10px gap) comes first (decision 32), so the cell starts at the amount column's left edge, 74px left of where the names start (226). With no batch it starts 6px after the heading's first letter (the row's padding). **That is the board's drawing, not a defect:** the boards draw it and the build matches them (0 off in 46,336 checks, decision 46), and it is decision 15's head. (2) *"As made" wraps and starts at the table's left edge.* The head row is a grid of the rows' four columns, but its three cells sit in DOM order (Ingredient, As made, % of batch), so As made, which belongs to the first column, is auto-placed on a second row under Ingredient; and the cell's `width: 1%` (a rule for the old table layout) makes its box 0.55px wide in a grid area, so "AS MADE" wraps to two lines (38px wide, ink from 6px after the heading's first letter, the table's left padding). The head is 52.17px tall (14.39 + 2 + 28.78 + 6 + 1). **That is also the board's drawing, by accident** (it reached the boards from their CSS, as decision 47 says), and it is the one thing the one-line head removes. Neither is a build defect against the sketch.
**The one-line head (drawn on the 17 wide boards).** The three cells take row 1, take their words' width and do not wrap: `.ingredient-table thead th { grid-row: 1; white-space: nowrap; width: auto; }`, and, because "AS MADE" is 59.45px wide in a 56px column, `.ingredient-table--as-made thead th.ingredient-table__col-numeric:not(:last-child) { justify-self: end; }` stands it on the column's right edge (the right edge of the figures under it), so it runs 3.45px left into the row's 6px padding and starts 2.56px inside the table's edge. Read in the build with the rule added (Olive Oil v1 at 1366): the head is **21.39px tall, 30.78px shorter** than the 52.17 (the decide row said about 16: that is the Ingredient row and its 2px gap; the wrapped As made's second line, 14.39px, is the rest); As made 82.56 to 142, Ingredient 152 to 232, % of batch right-aligned to the row's edge; Ingredient is 10px (the column gap) after As made, so "AS MADE INGREDIENT" reads as two labels close together; the same at 724, 744, 1024, 1600 and 1920. Without a batch the head is already one line (21.39px, as built); the rule changes only that one box (% of batch gets its words' width, no visible change).
**What moves.** With a batch in view, from 724 up, everything under the head moves up 30.78px: the table rows, the Step heads and the Total, then the Instructions and Before you start under them. Page heights: Olive Oil v1 31px shorter at every width 744 to 1920; Mexican Chocolate v3 31px shorter at 744 to 983 (one column) and **unchanged from 984**, where the tall side column sets the page height; there the grid's two rows share the 31px, so the empty space under its table grows by 15px (at 1024 from 352 to 368px, WebKit; decision 49 draws a fix for that space). Nothing above the head moves; no width moves. The Next version pen has no As made column, so its head is already one line (21.39px) and the pen boards and the remove-link board do not change.
**Redrawn:** `744-batch`, `834-batch`, `983-batch`, `984-batch`, `1024-batch`, `1366-batch`, `1600-batch`, `1920-batch`, `1600-long-history`, `724-1365-go-to-batch`, `1366-sticky-nav`, `984-sticky-nav`, `744-sticky-nav` and `1600-sticky-rail` (the head and the page heights, and the sticky boards' windows); `1600-no-batch`, `1600-pen` and `1600-remove-link` are regenerated and differ only by the inert rule. All 17 now draw the dist of 21:00:30, which carries two things the previous redraw did not (quicks 261004-szl and ta2): the Recorded row's date alone, and the menu button's own focus ring and the 10px radius on it and on the wordmark. **Conformance, board against build plus exactly the rule** (`INJECT=onehead node redraw-conform.mjs`, strict, WebKit, coarse: 11 boards, 33,106 checks, 0 off; the open fly-out, 609 checks, 0 off). Not individually checked: the three sticky boards, the rail board, the long-history board and the remove-link board (the same capture and CSS as checked ones).
**The option, drawn on `table-head-one-line.html`** (Olive Oil v1 with its batch at 1366, and Standard Base v2 with none at 1600; the build's head, A and B, each 1231 wide). **A (recommended; the 17 page boards draw it):** Ingredient stays over the amount and the names, as decision 15 drew it. It keeps the head over both columns it names, and with a batch it now reads as the column after As made; the cost is the 72px of indent Mark saw (6px with no batch). **B:** Ingredient starts where the names start (146px after the heading's first letter with a batch, 80px with none), 84px after As made; the amount column then has no word over it, which is not decision 15's two-columns-under-one-head. If Mark prefers B the page boards redraw with it (the section `ONEHEADB`).
**App change brief (for a `/gsd-quick`, not started; nothing under `app/` is edited).** In `app/src/styles/app.css`, in the `@media screen and (min-width: 724px)` block of the D3 grid, add `.ingredient-table thead th { grid-row: 1; white-space: nowrap; width: auto; }` and `.ingredient-table--as-made thead th.ingredient-table__col-numeric:not(:last-child) { justify-self: end; }`; the existing `white-space: nowrap` in the `thead th.ingredient-table__col-numeric:last-child` rule is then redundant. Print is untouched (the block is screen-only). Test first, in WebKit at 1366 with a batch (Olive Oil v1): the three head cells share one `y` and are 14.39px tall (As made's box is 59.45px wide, not 0.55), the head row is 21.39px tall, As made's right edge is the as-made column's (the figures' edge), and with no batch (Standard Base v2) and in the pen the head is unchanged at 21.39px. Read the real DOM before editing; the boards are the authority (`INJECT=onehead` conformance). If Mark chooses B, also set the name cell's `grid-column` to `3 / 4` with a batch and `2 / 3` without.
**Not verified:** nothing was tried on the iPad or the Mac's Safari; every reading is Playwright WebKit (coarse pointer, as the iPad) and system Chrome on a Mac; the wording "AS MADE INGREDIENT" reading as two labels is a look, not a measure.

(2) Look at the redrawn boards, with the head one line (1366 and 1024 are the iPad's). **Mark's answers (2026-10-05):** (1) A, Ingredient where it stands. (2) Looked (marked done, no note).
